# CLAUDE.md — Elysium Development Harness

> **14 October 2026, afternoon (WHAT THE PLAY-TEST FOUND — client v2.6.158 / server v2.6.46 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** One human (Claude, through the new rig) played a full game against two Expert bots on the released bundle, 8 rounds, Standard 3 s then Turn-based. The bots' findings (24, with timestamps) are `docs/handoffs/REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-14-playtest.md`; four were ours, fixed here, each pinned by a test that fails on v2.6.157. (1) **The default combatant went stale:** `_cmbLastBlock` outlived its turn, so `cmbAutoJoin` put a blocker from an earlier turn into two bots' combat (three times in one game, a third entry in the duel map). The defaults now die in `tallyStaleClose('turn')`, with a failed block (`cmbWatch`: a block counter that ends without a combat, stealth over intercept), with a finished combat, and with my own ✕ on a block (`avrClear`); and `cmbAutoJoin` stays out when another seat already faces the acting seat. (2) **The image fallback loop:** five `error` handlers set a fallback (a card back) on EVERY error; offline the back fails too, and each card-event clone re-requested it about 2,000 times a second for as long as it lived (the rig's CPU burn on a 2-core box). `imgFallbackOnce(img, src)` falls back once; the suite asserts no `()=>{ x.src=` error handler is left. (3) **A master played ON a vampire did not count:** `playOnActive` (now `async`) and the drop-onto-target path run `masterPlayGate`, like a play on the felt. (4) **The level chooser** took its default from ANY ready vampire (Lost in Crowds came up [OBF] for an obf-only Ashley because Norton had OBF): `_readyDiscSets(only)` and `modeChooseGate` follow `activeAnchor()`, the old rule without an anchor; and `parseV5Card` keeps `disciplines` (the v3 field the chooser reads -- a v5 database would have offered inferior with a warning every time). **Decided with Johan:** the human manages their own values by hand (combat blood, card costs, the Edge, pool); the bots manage theirs; 'X takes N (loses P blood, now M)' stays a report. The two START-HERE-BOTS.html sentences that promise more are the bot project's text (handoff §F). **New dev tool:** `client/playtest/` (playtest-rig.js, playtest.js, playtest-page.js, playtest-report.js, scenarios/, PLAYTEST.md) -- Playwright, never shipped; runs in `playtest/runs/` are gitignored. Gates: client 288/0 (5 new, all red on v2.6.157), the rest unchanged; a live run of the rig on v2.6.158: Blood Doll via Play on left the master count at 0, failing card backs gave 3-6 failed loads per 10 s instead of the loop.

> **14 October 2026 (THE TABLE LAYOUT AND THE DISCREET PANEL — client v2.6.157 / server v2.6.46 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** Johan's design question: the open dock covers the bottom quarter of the free board (202 of 719 px at 1366×768) and duplicates the pool (the globe) and the log (the left panel). Designed on a canvas (artifact "Handen i bordskanten"), then built over five test rounds he played offline (v2.6.152–156, branch `rim-preview`, never released). **Two choices, kept apart:** (1) **the room's Table layout** — Game settings (Full mode) / Play offline → *Large cards*; srv-2.6.46 stores `tableLayout:'large'` for a Classic room and echoes it like `tableSetup` (joined players+spectators, started, both took, snapshot). The client (`tableLarge()`, `tableLayoutSync()`) draws every free-board card at `L4_CARD_S` 0.6 (now a `let`), puts the suggested lower row at y 508 (`L4_SEAT.largeBottom`), the top row 8 px lower, and widens `pubBox()` by `L4_WIDE` 19 on both sides — the same for everyone, because positions are shared and a per-player card size (v2.6.152) drew the same cards at two sizes. A pure per-player zoom was measured and rejected: the felt is width-bound, so it gains 4–6 % on 16:9 and 0 % on 16:10. (2) **each player's discreet panel** (`conv.handRim`; the button `#l2dockMode` beside the pin, in the corner on the discreet panel; or Settings): crypt, library, hand, ash heap and pool as pockets along the bottom edge (`rimGeo`, `rimApply`, `rimHover`), each showing a strip, the one under the pointer rising; a pin `#rimPin` pins the hand's fan; the hand has no box and is centred on the table's middle. The board keeps `.l2dockopen`, so every dock rule for drops/drags holds; `setL2Dock` refuses to close; `#board.l4rim` hides the dock's chrome. **Measured** in headless Chromium: offline Large + discreet (draw, play, discard, pool ±, pin, toggle both ways), online three humans on srv-2.6.46 (identical sizes and globe spots on all screens, one on the discreet panel, two on the dock), srv-2.6.45 (Standard + the host's note), the pinned fan clear of both lower corner seats at 1909×1000, 1366×768, 1280×800. **Gates:** client 283/0 (5 red against v2.6.151), dispatch 68/0 (3 red against srv-2.6.45), protocol 6/0, canary 57/0, endpoint 24/0, soak control 0, B1 byte-identical, embed both pages, check-versions green. **Not tested:** touch; Lite mode (the Table layout row is Full-only); a dock player at a Large table has the lower row under the open dock (by design, said in the tooltip). Handoff: `docs/handoffs/REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-14-table-layout.md`.

> **13 October 2026, night (THE EDGE ON THE GLOBE — client v2.6.151 / server srv-2.6.45 unchanged / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge; ships with v2.6.150 in PR #9):** Johan's 'kör' on the three small open items. (1) **The Edge on the globe:** `l4GlobeEdges()` toggles `.l4gedgeon` on every free-board globe — mine when `state.edge` and no own token (`conv.edgeToken && tokens.get('edge')`), another seat's when its pub says `edge` and carries no `edge` token; called from `updateL4Opponents`, `renderL4Globes`, `updateEdge`, `ensureEdgeToken` and `removeEdgeToken`. The `.l4gedge` chip (top right of the circle) is in the first build and added to older globes on repaint. Run with a human and a socket bot on the real server: Anna's globe marks when her token setting is off and the token takes over when it is on; the bot's globe marks 16 s into the game. This closes the Classic review's §5 for the bot project. (2) **Ordinals** in `statRow`'s seat column, `renderRemote`'s title and `#pseat` (`ordinal(...)`). (3) **Help → Local server and START-HERE.html** mention the suggested table setup (`embed-help.js server` run; the Host Guide lists no room settings and was left alone — its generator still says v1.8 against the v1.9 docx). **Gates:** client 279/0 (1 new), protocol 6/0, dispatch 65/0, canary 57/0, embed `--check` both pages, B1 byte-identical, check-versions green. **Not run:** the mark at a bot table under a Hold on; touch.

> **13 October 2026, late evening (THE TAB SAYS SO — client v2.6.150 / server srv-2.6.45 unchanged / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge):** Johan's three adjustments after deploying v2.6.149, seen at the live table. (1) **The seat chip under the globe** (`.l4gseat` is its own line after `.l4gpool`; the name line holds the name alone; both the first build and the repaint in `updateL4GlobeHTML` write it). At the lower row the chip ends 182 px above the open hand panel at 1366 × 768. (2) **A resolver opens nothing on other screens.** Johan: each player chooses whether to pin the tray. `tally` no longer calls `pinPlayedAuto`; `avrRender` toggles `#playedTab.live` (a pulse, none under reduced motion) and writes the mode into `#playedTabAvr` for players with that helper on; the hover peek still stays open while a resolver runs (`hidePlayedSoon`), and a combat the player starts from the card menu still shows and then removes the tray (`cmbEnsure` → `pinPlayedAuto`). **The suggested top row is back at the top edge** (`T.y + m` = 25; `L4_SEAT.topY` is gone; four seat-spot test values moved back). (3) **The title** `<title>` and `og:title`: 'Elysium: a digital VTES table'. > **13 October 2026, evening (THE FACE DECIDES — client v2.6.149 / server v2.6.45 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** Johan merged PR #6 and said 'kör' on K1–K6 from the bot review, plus anything reasonable found on the way; bots stay Structured-only (lobby and offline already enforce it; a CLI socket bot can still be pointed at a Classic room on purpose — that is how the bot project will test its fix). **Built, measured with the same script on v2.6.148 + srv-2.6.44 and on the new pair with two real browser clients (headless Chromium, real mouse for drags, double-clicks and the card menu) — except where the old version cannot reach the same state (K2: on v2.6.148 the drag had already taken the vampire out of torpor; the felt clock: new pair only; the group drop of torpor cards: unit test only; the transfer figures call the client's own counter with the phase set to Influence, not through the phase bar):** (K1) on the free board a crypt card's FACE decides between `ready` and `uncontrolled` — `freeFaceZone` in `move()` (coordinates follow what the caller ASKED: a drag passes display coords, `opts.disp`), `freeFaceSync` after every flip (double-click, card menu, the controller's `ctrl` flip, Flip face up selected, Flip selected); torpor is never touched by it. Measured before → after: a face-down card dragged `ready`/MINION 1 → `uncontrolled`/MINION 0; a vampire double-clicked where it lies `uncontrolled` (menu: Card action only) → `ready` (Actions + Combat); +1 blood in Influence on the face-down card cost no pool → costs 1, on the vampire in play cost 1 → costs none. Minion counts skip face-down cards (a belt for older pubs); a dragged uncontrolled or torpored crypt card never attaches; `restoreFromPub` and `given` pass the face INTO the move (a fresh card is face down — the rule would otherwise file a face-up vampire as uncontrolled); `enterL4` puts an older save right (`freeFaceSyncAll`); the crypt park lines say 'on the table'. (K2) card menu ▸ Leave torpor on the free board: where it lies (was the felt's top-left corner). (K3) torpor survives a drag on the free board (`dz`, single and group drop); a TORPOR band + dimmed art on every seat's screen (own: one pass in `layout()`; others: `addRCard`). (K4) `advanceTurnCounter` writes the felt label, and the match clock's tick, its end and its clearing write the felt's centre clock — both were written only when the felt was rebuilt ('Round 1 · Turn 1' all game). (K5) `pinPlayedAuto`: a tray a resolver opened (anyone's tally, a combat from the card menu) closes when the resolver closes (`avrRender` with no mode → `playedAutoClose`); the player's own pin stays. `playedAutoPin` is declared beside `avr` — `avrRender` runs at start-up, and declared beside `playedPinned` the page died in the TDZ (the harness caught it). The suggested top row starts at y 132 (`L4_SEAT.topY`; it was 25): with an empty tray nothing of the top seat is covered at 1366 × 768 (tray bottom 202 px, own globe 222 px). (K6) `clearL4Globes` no longer removes `#l4oppWrap` — every `renderL4` (a roster change, a window resize, a settings apply, the pool-colour toggle) took the other seats' cards down and nothing drew them again until the next board — and `renderL4Globes` ends with `updateL4Opponents()` (globes from their pubs, cards reconciled, not rebuilt); an offline seat's globe is greyed with 'offline' under it (`glob.off`). Measured: Anna dropped → v2.6.148 showed 0 of her 4 cards for the whole drop, v2.6.149 shows 4 and the mark. **Found on the way, built:** (a) THE COUNT COMES BACK — `joined` zeroed `net.turnNo`, so after any reconnect or reload the turn line restarted at 'Round 1 · Turn 1' and `openingXfMalus` gave 1 transfer again (measured on v2.6.148: Johan at Round 3 · Turn 5 reconnected, and at the next turn his line said 'Round 1 · Turn 1' while Anna's said 'Round 3 · Turn 6'; a reload did the same). An automatic reconnect into the same room keeps the count; every pub carries `tn`/`rn`; right after joining a running game (`net.tnSync`, until the next `turn`) the client takes the highest whole count (`tn` AND `rn`) from the boards, its own `joined.myPub` first (`tnAdopt`); a seat taken over or a loaded match (`took`) takes the count from the board handed over. The server's `sanitizePub` is a whitelist and dropped both — srv-2.6.45 keeps them (positive, ≤ 9999). Measured on the new pair: reconnect R2 T4 kept, reload R2 T4 taken, the next turn R3 T5 on both; a spectator arriving at R3 T6 reads R3 T6 (v2.6.148: R1 T0). (b) `pubRiders`: another seat's attached cards ride on their host in all three remote renderers (`renderL4OppCards`, `buildMat`, `renderFull`), torpor and uncontrolled hosts included — a torpored vampire's equipment vanished from every other screen on both board styles — and a card listed in a host's `attached` without a host of its own (the released bot's equipment) is drawn on that host, not in a corner (bot rig, both styles, final build: the equipment 24–63 px off its vampire — the stack's own steps — instead of 488 px away on Classic and the ready region's corner on Structured); kids loops skip ids left in `attached` for cards in the ash heap or burned, and a snapshot restore puts a torpored vampire's equipment back on it. Also: a released or detached face-down crypt rider follows the face rule. **Visible changes beyond the K-list:** minion counts skip face-down crypt cards on Structured too (one parked face down in ready no longer counts); flipping a ready vampire face down on the free board takes it out of play; Stack selected takes ready cards only, so face-down crypt cards (now uncontrolled) are not stacked; a Played-tray peek that a live resolver held open closes when the resolver ends unless the pointer is on it. **An independent reviewer** read the diff and the claims: seven code findings, all fixed and pinned — one a regression (a client still retrying a lost server that joined ANOTHER room by hand carried the old count in), plus a loaded match leaving the host's count behind, a released face-down rider staying in ready, the felt clock missing its last tick, a hover peek outliving its resolver, every renderL4 rebuilding the other seats' cards, and a `tn` without `rn` resetting the round; also out-of-play riders drawn on hosts and a restore leaving a torpored vampire's equipment loose. Fixing the hover peek hit the TDZ a second time (`playedPinned`, read by `avrRender` at start-up) — the tray's state is now declared beside `avr`. **Checked and left:** offline bots — `syncOffBotLock` already forces Structured (a start-gate belt contradicted the v2.6.93 test that pins the design; reverted); the board style cannot change after creation; `loadMatch` spawns no bots. **Gates:** client 278/0 (7 new tests + 4 seat-spot values moved to y 132; against v2.6.148 11 red), dispatch 65/0 (1 new; against srv-2.6.44 1 red), protocol 6/0, canary 57/0, error endpoint 24/0, soak `--control` exit 0 and a three-bot Structured soak (12 and, on the final build, 14 turns; 0 stuck), B1 byte-identical, both help pages up to date, check-versions green. **Handoff:** `docs/handoffs/REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-13-v2.6.149.md` (K1 shipped — zones on the free board are honest; the top row's new formula; the client now draws equipment on its vampire; `tn`/`rn`). **Measured at four window sizes (the top-centre seat of two, while a resolver holds the tray open):** 1920 × 1080 and 1366 × 768 — an empty tray covers nothing (1366: a row of cards in it covers 28 % of the globe and a third of the cards); 1280 × 720 — empty: only the name line's bottom 5 px, with a card row about half; 1024 × 600 — empty: the name line and about half the globe and cards, with a card row all of it. The tray closes with the resolver. **Not run:** touch; a referee watching; six humans at once; a socket bot at a Classic table after the change.

> **13 October 2026, afternoon (THE FREE BOARD, READ FOR THE BOTS — a review, no code; client v2.6.148 / server v2.6.44 / bot v0.6.198 unchanged; nothing to deploy):** Johan: 'review the bot code with the client's eyes' — is anything in the way of bots at Classic tables? `bot/` read and run, not touched. **The bot's side, ranked** (handoff `docs/handoffs/REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-13-classic-review.md`): (1) **the hard stop** — on a Classic table `_openSlot` never returns once a camp row has used its distinct slots (the clamp at x 908 makes later indices collide; 4 slots at the right-hand seats, 5–11 elsewhere). Run: a unit probe; our soak per board style with a measuring guard (7 saturations at one six-bot table) and without it (6 of 6 Classic tables stopped breathing after 27–50 s, the same decks on Structured finished in 170 s); live tables on the real server with three bots on sockets (the seat-2 bot stopped in its third turn in one run and in its FIRST in the traced one — Effective Management with four uncontrolled cards in a four-slot row; the table sees nothing for a minute and a half, then 'disconnected' when the server reaps the silent socket, and the host's ⏭ Pass their turn… / 💀 Oust this player… get it going again); and **main's pair today (v2.6.145 + srv-2.6.43) with lobby bots in a Classic room: the host's page stopped answering (272 s into one game, in the bot's first turn in the next)** — a lobby bot runs in the host's tab, a second human at that table saw every seat online and the turn on the bot for the 150 s watched, and after the host's tab was closed nobody had a tool left. v2.6.148 closes that path. An independent reader checked the handoff's ~190 statements against the code and the evidence files before it went out; its corrections are in. (2) Equipment carries no `host`, so it is drawn in the corner, not on its vampire — on Structured too. (3) Permanents at fixed felt points (64, 64 / y 40): on the free board every bot's pile lands on the same corner, where a human sits. (4) The camp steps 96 px for 42 px cards and sits under both panels. (5) No Edge token on the felt. **Ours, found on the way, humans only — analysed, NOT built, waiting for 'kör':** **K1** on the free board `zone` drifts from the card: a dragged face-down crypt card becomes `ready` (MINION 1, 🧛1, its blood in Influence is no transfer), a vampire flipped where it lies stays `uncontrolled` (MINION 0, no clan symbol, +1 blood costs a pool as a transfer) — the second is the ordinary move since v2.6.146 placed the cards at the seat; proposal: the FACE decides the zone, a drag never does. **K2** card menu ▸ Leave torpor throws the vampire to the felt's top-left corner. **K3** a torpored card dragged becomes `ready`. **K4** the felt's Round/Turn label never leaves 'Round 1 · Turn 1' (also on v2.6.145). **K6** every other seat's cards vanish from the free board when the roster changes (`renderL4()` draws them through `layout()`, then `renderL4Globes()` → `clearL4Globes()` removes their layer and nothing redraws); the next board from anyone, or the viewer's own next card move, brings them back — 0.6 s at a table with another live human, but at a two-player table Anna's cards stayed gone from Johan's felt for the 60 s watched after her tab closed, until he dragged a card of his own (also on main's pair); and the felt does not show that a seat is offline at all — only the Table panel and the log, both folded by default there. **K5 — my miss in v2.6.146:** the seat spots were measured against the hand panel only; the Played tray covers the top row (1366 × 768: canonical x 131–823, y 24–127, to 156 with cards — the whole top-centre seat), and anyone's resolver PINS it on every helper user's screen, where it stays after Resolve. Proposal: an auto-opened tray closes with its resolver, and the top row moves to y 132. **Docs only in this commit.** Scratch probes are not in the repo; the handoff carries a six-line script that shows the stop.

> **13 October 2026, midday (A SEAT FOR THE WATCHER — client v2.6.148 / server v2.6.44 unchanged / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge; ships with v2.6.146–147 in the same PR — together: restart the server, purge the client):** Johan: 'kör' on (b)–(e), and for (a): bots on Structured tables only for now, that is where he has tested them under controlled conditions. **Built, each with a test that fails on v2.6.147 and a run against the real server:** (1) *Spectators.* `schedulePush`, `pushNow` and `mpRelay` send nothing while `net.spect` (the debounced push checks again when it fires — one armed before the client knew it was watching still went out). Measured after: 0 notices and 0 `board` / `log` frames in 20 s of watching, both board styles (before: 53 in 35 s). `mpWatching()` keeps the welcome layer off a running game someone watches. (2) *Arriving.* `mpLandAtTable()` closes the Join dialog and switches to `mpLandView()`: a player's home view; for a watcher the shared felt on a free board, and on a Structured table a visit to the seat whose turn it is (entered through the home frame — from the start view a visit drew bare felt). Called by `joined` (not on an automatic reconnect, which keeps the view it had — run while visiting a seat) and by `started` for everyone, spectators too; `goHome` leads a watcher to the same place. (3) *The snapshot restore* (`restoreFromPub`) on a free-board table takes ready, uncontrolled and torpor positions as published; the Structured path is untouched. (4) *The globe.* `joined` reads the own `pool` token from `myPub` into `net._myGlobeAt`; `renderL4Globes` uses it for the own globe before any default; `started` and leaving clear it. Run: globe and one card dragged, tab reloaded — Resume + Join and Join + snapshot restore both come back with every position and no Esc. (5) *Bots.* `netBotWhy` reads `net.boardMode`: Add bot is offered in Structured rooms only (run in both lobbies). The `tableSetupOn()` bot guard stays as the safety net for a bot on its own socket; the handoff now says the seat ask is for later. Also: a spectator's join line no longer says 'seat undefined'. **Left out on purpose:** the helicopter view for a watcher on a Structured table (it still draws the watcher as a seat); a watcher's view does not follow the turn on its own; `demoted` (a player moved to spectator mid-game) still drops to the start view — Esc now leads to the table. Gates: client 271/0 with v0.6.198 beside (no skips; destructive vs v2.6.147: 5 red = the five new tests), dispatch 64/0, protocol 6/0, canary 57/0, endpoint 24/0, soak control exit 0, B1 byte-identical 1 516 413, embed --check both pages, check-versions green.

> **13 October 2026, later (THE PIN STANDS — client v2.6.147 / server v2.6.44 unchanged / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; pure client, purge; ships with v2.6.146 in the same PR — together: restart the server, purge the client):** Johan on the dock pin: more discreet — the needle lying or slanted while the panel is loose, upright when it is pinned 'so it looks fastened', and the words in a tooltip. **Built:** `#l2dockPin` is an icon button, 22 × 16 px in the same corner: one inline SVG path, a pushpin drawn point down. CSS lays it on its side (`rotate(90deg)`, point to the left) and `.on` stands it up; a 160 ms turn carries the change, none under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `setDockPin` sets `title` to 'Pinned' / 'Not pinned' and no longer rewrites the button's markup; `aria-label` 'Keep the panel open' is the constant name, `aria-pressed` the state. **Two choices, both looked at before they were made:** lying, not slanted — at 12 px on an ordinary-density screen a 45° needle is a smudge and a 90° one is crisp (three shapes at 45° and at 90° were rendered and enlarged pixel for pixel); an SVG, not the pushpin emoji — that glyph's own tilt differs from font to font, and a turn has to start from a known angle. **Run in the browser:** a real mouse at 1024×600, 1280×720 (1× and 2× density) and 1920×1080, Classic and Structured — the button is hit, the needle turns, the tooltip follows, unpinning by the button lets the panel fold, a click on the panel's background still pins. Every v2.6.146 browser run was repeated on these bytes. **Johan's question: fix the five weaknesses before the handoff goes to the bot project? Analysed and proposed, NOT built — waiting for 'kör'.** Only (a) touches the bots: whether bots may sit at Classic tables online decides what the handoff asks of them, so that decision comes first. (b)–(e) are client-only and independent of the bot project. **Reading the code for the proposal found what (b) really is — spectating is broken today:** (1) a spectator's client pushes its (empty) board like a player's (`schedulePush` has no `net.spect` gate); the server answers 'Spectators can only chat.', the client logs and toasts it, and the log line itself schedules the next push (`log` → `scheduleSave` → `schedulePush`) — an echo that never stops. Measured with the real server: **53 notices in about 35 seconds of only watching** a Classic table (35 on Structured), and the same on v2.6.145 + srv-2.6.43. (2) The welcome layer covers the table (item (b) as first reported). (3) On a Structured table a spectator's home view is its own empty board, and the helicopter view draws a spectator as a player with an empty slot — one of two players was not drawn at all. Seat visits (the number keys) work. So Classic needs three small things (no pushes from a spectator, the welcome layer off, the view switched); Structured needs a landing view chosen (proposed: a visit to the seat whose turn it is). The proposal, item by item, is in the journal's newest entry. Gates: client 266/0 with v0.6.198 beside (no skips; destructive vs v2.6.146: 2 red = the new test + the v2.6.146 pin test, whose label line now reads the tooltip), dispatch 64/0, protocol 6/0, canary 57/0, endpoint 24/0, soak control exit 0, B1 byte-identical 1 512 026, embed --check both pages, check-versions green.

> **13 October 2026 (THE TABLE IS SET — client v2.6.146 / server v2.6.44 / bot v0.6.198 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** the player's follow-up from the night before ('no spaces designated to players, everybody started to move the globes, same with the crypt cards'), built the way Johan chose it. **The room option 'Suggested table setup'** — under Game settings in Create game and in Play offline, ticked by default, Classic tables only. With it every seat's pool globe STARTS at its own spot around the table in turn order (Lackey's layout, clockwise from the top left; the lower row sits just above the open hand panel — Johan tried a compromise position and chose 'everything above the panel'), and the first four crypt cards are laid face down beside the globe at the deal. It is a placement help for the first step only: afterwards everything moves as before and a later draw lands on the centre row as it always did. Unticked: exactly the old table. The globe's seat chip keeps the chair and says 1st, 2nd … on every Classic table. **Also (Johan):** the dock pin sits in the hand panel's top right corner and the pinned edge is a thin gold line instead of a glow. **On the wire:** ONE optional field, `tableSetup`. `create` may set it; srv-2.6.44 keeps it for a Classic room and a strict `true`; `joined` (players and spectators), `started` and both `took` messages carry it; the room snapshot round-trips it. The server does nothing else — the Classic deal is unchanged, and the four cards are four ordinary `drawCrypt` verbs the client sends at `dealt` and places as the `drew` answers arrive. So an older client at the table loses nothing (it sets up by hand), and a new client on an older server says so and starts the old way. Both pairs were run. **A seated bot switches the setup off for the whole table** (`tableSetupOn()`): the bot camps on its own ellipse, and 14 of 70 bot-seat / human-seat pairs would overlap. The humans get one log line after the deal. Classic is the default board style and bots can be added to a Classic lobby (see (a) below), so this is the usual online bot table, not a corner. > **23 September 2026 (THE COMBAT STEPS CARRY STATE — client v2.6.129 / server v2.6.39 / bot v0.6.162 beside the suite; purge the client AND restart the server):** Johan's step-driven combat helper, rulebook-checked against VEKN's rulebook (4. Detailed Turn Sequence). **The steps stay the rulebook's seven** (`AVR_PHASES` was already exactly right; Johan's list had Additional strikes as a step and no Damage resolution — additional strikes are extra choose/resolve pairs INSIDE Strike, damage is its own step), so `ph` keeps its meaning on the wire. **Panel:** in combat the `#avrLive` row becomes a clickable rail (`cmbRail`: R#, seven step chips, range pill; the a/b counters move out via the new `#avrCnt` wrapper), and `#avrDuel` gets a STAGE between the two combatants (`cmbStage`: Set range this/next round, the Close/Long switch, damage a vs b with ±, Next strike pair, the Ends/Continues switch, the outcome) plus per-side controls in each panel (`cmbPanelCtl`: Maneuver, Strike ▾ / + Additional, the Damage read-out Incoming · prevent ± · Burns X → Y left / wounded → torpor, Press). `avrPhase` follows the rules: a set range (`rl`) skips Determine range, a combat-ends strike (`ce`) jumps to End of round, End of round's Next = `Round N ▸` (uncancelled press to continue → `cmbNewRound`) or `End combat` (→ `avrResolve`); ◂ no longer crosses back into the previous round. A lone combatant who is not the turn seat now waits on the RIGHT (`cmbSidesIn`). **Menu:** the ready minion's right-click menu gets `Combat` right after Actions (`cmbMenu`: Maneuver to X, Set range ▸ this/next round, Strike ▸ six, Additional strike…, Prevent damage…, Press to continue/end) — same functions as the panel, `noUndo`, no lock, no batch close. A control used with no combat open opens one and announces that vampire (`cmbEnsure`); with the helper off only the log line goes out. **Lines:** `CMB_LINE` builds every line; strike / steal / dodge / combat-ends / maneuver / press / taken are the bot's own shapes (verified against the released bot's L12 + `_parseCombatNarration` in a `testB`), `sets range to X[ next round]`, `gets an additional strike (CARD)`, `prevents N damage (CARD)` are new (PROTOCOL §12). **Damage is never applied to the board** (Johan: the players keep track). Passing Damage resolution (or resolving from Strike/Damage) makes EACH client log its OWN combatant's `takes N (loses P blood, now M)[ and goes to torpor].` once per round (`cmbWatch` → `cmbLogTaken`, key serial:round:seat), from the blood snapshot taken on ENTERING Damage (`cmbSnapNow`) — a player who burns blood mid-step does not skew it; × withdraw never logs. **Wire:** combat tally fields `rng rl nx pr sp ce sa sb xa xb pa pb`; the human client sends them as PARTIAL writes (`cmbSet` → `cmbSend`) beside the unchanged whole-state `avrPush` (a/b/ph/rd). srv-2.6.39 `tallyCombat` merges them (absent = preserve, a changed `rd` = fresh round with `nx` carried into `rng` + `rl` 1, mode change = fresh, only combat broadcasts carry them); the client's `tallyCombatMerge` (MP handler + `hotBridge.tallyIn`) is the twin, and a suite test compares both on 400 random writes (mutant-checked). `a`/`b` are now per ROUND. `avrDuelAnnounce` online writes the own seat optimistically. `avrFresh(mode)` replaces the six `avr={…}` literals. **Gates:** client 217/0 with bot v0.6.162 beside (+8 tests; the 7 combat ones red on v2.6.128; the stale v2.6.126 LIVE test fixed — it listened on `chat`, the bot's notes ride `log` since v0.6.114), dispatch 51/0 (+1, red on srv-2.6.38), protocol 6/0 (the lint now follows `tallyCombat(m`/`tallyCombatMerge(m` to their SOURCE key lists — red with the old doc), canary 37/0, error-endpoint 21/1 (cardfx.json not beside — expected), B1 byte-identical 1 438 501 (built to /tmp/rebuilt.html — see learnings), check-versions green. **Not done: a browser look** (stage width 190 px, rail wrap in a narrow overlay). **Bot follow-up:** `spec-bot-combat-fields-2026-09-23.md`.

> **22 September 2026, latest (BOT-CARD CONTROLS AT A HOTSEAT + BURN — client v2.6.126 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.151, pure client — purge the client; srv and bot unchanged, bot floor stays v0.6.147):** the bot project's `spec-bot-card-ctrl-2026-09-22.md`, implemented as specified. Johan hit it first: at a hotseat he wanted to LOCK a bot's vampire (a card the bot could not model had locked it) and the menu row did nothing. **The gap, read in source:** `avRenderCard`'s contextmenu routes every bot-card verb to `hotCtrl` offline, and `hotCtrl` returned silently for two independent reasons — the bot seat's stored blob is the v2.6.122 placeholder (no cards → `!k`), and a bot's pub carries no `owner` (→ the owner check fails even when a card is found). Nothing else carried `ctrl` over the bridge (`grep t:'ctrl'` found only `ctrlOnline`). Seat-level verbs were fine since v2.6.97; the card-level ones were the hole. **Fix A:** `hotCtrl` starts with `if(hotBotSeat(seat))` → `hotBridge.toBot(seat, {t:'ctrl', cid, act (16-clamped), from})` — srv 927's exact shape — then returns; the bot applies, clamps and pushes, its publish is the truth. Human seats keep the old body untouched (no bridge). **Fix B:** a `🔥 Burn (remove from game)` row on a bot's card menu (`_botSeat && !_mine`, `noUndo`, behind `uiConfirm`) sending `_do('burn')`, so it works in BOTH modes — the bot's `ctrl 'burn'` receiver (v0.6.129, their debt 26h) had no sender anywhere; diablerie of a bot's torpored vampire has a verb now. The human runs the blood-hunt referendum; the bot does not call one. **Shared optimism:** `ctrlOptimistic(seat,cid,act)` factored out of `ctrlOnline`, used by both routes, handles `burn` (splices the card and anything with `host===cid`, as the bot drops attachments). **Tests:** one sync test on the fake bridge (`parityTable` + a 4th HUMAN seat — see the learning about `hotReRender` re-persisting the active human's blob) pins the wire shape, holder-only delivery, the 16 clamp, optimistic lock/blood/burn, the untouched human path incl. the owner refusal, and the Burn row in source; destructive against the DELIVERED v2.6.125 it fails on the first assertion (bridge heard nothing). > **21 September 2026 (AN OUST PAYS THE LIVING AND MOVES THE TURN — client v2.6.123 / server v2.6.38 / bot v0.6.150, SERVER RESTART + purge; ships together with client v2.6.123 and bot v0.6.150):** the two server items of the bot project's 21 Sep spec, taken at Johan's word so online and offline close in ONE deploy. **(1) The live predator.** `bounty` and `hostOust` took `(i - 1 + n) % n` without asking whether that seat was still in the game. Shown LIVE before the fix (real server, three real bots): seat 2 out, seat 3 falls → `B got bounty from C`, roster `2:out/vp1`, the real predator's pool +0. After: `A got bounty from C`, `vp2`, +6. `prevLiveSeat()` skips `out` and deliberately NOT `vacant` — a vacant seat is still in the game. Nobody left → no VP, and the sys line no longer names a recipient. **(2) The turn.** An oust of the seat that HOLDS the turn hands it on (`turnOnFromOusted` → `nextLiveSeat`, broadcast AFTER the roster so clients learn why before what), exactly as `vacateSeat` has always done. **The protocol doc already said `bounty` "advances the turn if needed"** — the doc promised it and the server never did; the lint cannot see that (it diffs names, not behaviour). **(3) Found by running it:** with (2) in place the ousted bot's own late `pass` (bot v0.6.140+) drew `err: It is not your turn.` into every such trace. A pass from a seat that is `out` and does not hold the turn is now ignored silently; a LIVE seat passing out of turn still errs (v2.5.64). The turn is never advanced twice — asserted in the unit test and seen live (`C saw turn -> [2]`). **Probe hygiene, relearned:** the server writes `elysium-rooms.json` beside ITSELF (`DATA_DIR = __dirname`), so a live probe leaves a snapshot in the workdir and the next run dies on `That room name is taken` — delete both json files between runs (CLAUDE.md has said so since June) and never let them into a delivery. And `pkill -f <port>` kills the shell whose command line contains that port. > **21 September 2026 (THE ORDER WAS LUCK — client v2.6.123 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.150, pure client — purge; supersedes v2.6.122, deploy this one):** Johan's standing "anything odd?" gate, answered by re-reading the diff and RUNNING the one path the v2.6.122 suite had only driven with fake seats. **(1) The host-oust order was the opposite of what the comment said.** `hostOustHot` told the bot first (`forceOust`), and the bot's `send()` → `_drain()` is SYNCHRONOUS while its token bucket has tokens, so its pool-0 board fanned out inside that call: the predator saw `board > oust-inferred > bounty > roster`. The +6 came out right only because bot v0.6.147's no-server net records the name and swallows the verb that follows — correctness borrowed from the other project's safety net, under a comment and three doc entries claiming we had ordered it ourselves. Now `awardBountyHot` + `hotRosterPush` run BEFORE `forceOust`; measured live: `bounty > roster > board`, no inference. The v2.6.122 notes below are corrected by this entry: the 'synchronously ahead of the board' claim held for a HUMAN's oust (600 ms debounce) and not for a bot's. **(2) A give to an ousted bot destroyed the card.** The bot's `given` receiver returns early when `out`; `giveHotToBot` sent the verb and destroyed our copy regardless. Refused with a toast now, before anything is touched. **(3) gid was cut at 16** — the tally duel cid's cap, copied onto the wrong verb; the server's `cleanId` strips `[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]` and keeps 32. **(4) The registry promise was half kept:** the bot file loaded only at Start / Add bot, so a deck the client has never heard of could not appear in a menu until after the first spawn. `hotBotMenuPreload()` loads it quietly when a seat becomes a bot and when the host's lobby renders; failures stay silent there because the spawn path owns the dialog (and the loader forgets a failed attempt). **Looked at and left:** the idle dialog stays open if the bot passes while it is showing (its buttons are then no-ops — `fin` is once-only; `uiDialog` has no programmatic close); `hotBotOusted` recomputes the predator from `net.roster` rather than trusting the bridge's `pred` argument (all five `out` writers call `hotRosterPush`, census: 7147/7205/7217/7258/8300, so the two books cannot disagree today); a bot's `res:1` tally runs the human's own Resolve-clear setting, as online. Gates: client 201/0 with the bot (+2; destructive 2/2 vs the DELIVERED v2.6.122), canary 37/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1307218, check-versions green.

> **21 September 2026 (THE PARITY ROUND — client v2.6.122 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.150, pure client — purge; bundle bot v0.6.150 + the re-issued START-HERE-BOTS.html):** the bot project's handover `spec-hotseat-parity-2026-09-21.md`, items 1–6 (client parts) + the gate it asked for. Measured by them THROUGH our bridge: 4 of 4 tables froze on an ousted bot, 0 stealth cards in 35 block attempts, no bot ever learned anybody left the game. **The design move:** `hotBridge` plays the SERVER's role, so it now keeps the server's three pieces of table state ITSELF — `tally` (room.tally), `_out`, `_vp` — with `tallyIn()`, `predOf()`, `rosterMsg()/pushRoster()/syncRoster()`. Self-contained on purpose: their harness lifts the `hotBridge` literal + `hotFanOut` VERBATIM, and a relay that needed `net.roster` or a client function would silently not run there (verified: the lifted literal relays tally/bounty/roster/openHand/give with nothing else defined). Everything client-shaped is `typeof`-guarded (`hotBotOusted`, `hotGivenToHuman`, `MP_HANDLERS`). **(1) The sweep** skips roster-`out` bots (also ones ousted DURING the sweep: no toast, no camera, no turn) and `hotAwaitPass()` replaces the bare await: an IDLE clock (`HOT_PASS_IDLE_MS` 3 min from the bot's last bridge message `rec.lastAt`; stands still while anyone holds) that ASKS — it never advances by itself, because a bot is legitimately silent while a human resolves a combat by hand and a Hold has no timer by design; Cancel re-arms. The same exit is `⏭ Pass their turn…` on the seat menu offline (`hotForcePass`), the twin of `hostPass`. A table torn down mid-sweep leaves the promise unresolved on purpose. **(2) tally:** `GAME_HANDLERS.tally` field for field (absent fields preserved while the mode is unchanged, mode change fresh, duel merged per seat, `res` relayed never stored), to every bot seat INCLUDING the sender and into `MP_HANDLERS.tally` for the human; the human's `avrPush`, both clear/resolve sends and the duel announce go through `hotTallySend()` as `who:<human>` (not echoed back into the handler — it would re-run a resolve's clear). All self-gated on `hotBotsSeated()`: a human-only hotseat is what it was. **(3) bounty + roster:** the bridge's `bounty` case marks out, pays the LIVE predator and pushes the roster; `awardBountyHot` pays where the pool LIVES — the verb to a bot, **the live state to the ACTIVE human (a pre-existing loss: the active seat's blob is stale while it plays, so a host-oust bounty to the device holder vanished at the next hand-off; with one human + bots it is the normal case)**, the blob (+ pub) to a stored human. `hotRosterPush()` at all five `out` sites, synchronously AHEAD of the pool-0 board so bot v0.6.147's no-server net sees `out` already set; the other order (bot self-oust: board first) is covered by the bot's own de-duplication — the LIVE test asserts +6 exactly once. **(4) decks:** `HOT_BOT_DECKS` is `{key,label}` over `ElysiumBot.ARCHETYPES`; both spawn paths pass `deckName`; + V5 Ventrue; the three embedded V5 lists (list-identical, checked) are gone; unknown registry keys appear under the deck's own name once the bot is loaded; `wall` hidden (retired 4 Aug). Selection is stored by KEY (`botDeckKey`, `#lobBotDeck` values). Needs bot ≥ v0.6.147 beside the client — `hotBotDeckFor` throws a readable reason into both spawn-failure dialogs. **(5) levels:** `HOT_BOT_PERSONAS` expert/experienced/novice, `HOT_BOT_PERSONA='expert'` at all four fallback sites, the toggle's first click lands on Expert, lobby select values AND labels. **(6) relays:** `openHand` (+revoke) to the human's receiver and the other bots; `give` → the server's whitelisted `given` + `gave`; human → bot `giveHotToBot` (no undo entry: the bot has no `recall` receiver). **Not in this round (server, needs a restart → srv-2.6.38):** `GAME_HANDLERS.bounty`/`hostOust` take the ring predecessor without skipping `out` seats; the turn is not handed on when its holder is ousted. **For the bot project (read, not run):** `_onGiven` matches `attachTo` against the vampire's NAME, the wire carries a CID (srv 863 / client `given`) — a human's Fame/Pentex drop probably never arms its hooks online either. A bot that ousted itself has no un-oust receiver. Gates: client 199/0 with the bot beside (+9 tests; destructive 9/9 vs v2.6.121 — eight fail, and the sweep test reproduces THE FREEZE: the pristine client never returns), canary 37/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, endpoint 21/0 (+1: cardfx.json not beside), B1 byte-identical 1 305 927, check-versions green. Browser check before purge: 1 human + 2 bots to an oust (Played-tab counters moving on a bot's block, +6 on your own oust of a bot, the round continuing), the Debug hand offline, the six decks and three levels in both lobbies.

> **17 September 2026 (ENTER STAYS WITH THE FOCUSED CONTROL — client v2.6.119 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** Johan's standing "anything odd?" gate on v2.6.118, before deploy. `chatFocus` (Enter) was gated only on the chat line being visible — so a player who had tabbed to or clicked a button (Menu, Pass, ▶) and pressed Enter to activate it would have had the key prevented and the caret sent to the chat instead. `chatFocusWanted()` is the gate now: chat line visible AND no button/link/select/summary/label holds focus; a bare focus (body / the felt) still hands Enter to the chat. > **17 September 2026 (FIVE FROM THE FEEDBACK ROUND — client v2.6.118 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** five items from the players' feedback on the clients, all client-side. **(1) Enter focuses the chat line** when it is not already active: a rebindable action `chatFocus` (Shortcuts › Advanced, default Enter) gated by `chatVisible()` — where there is no chat line (solo) the key is not even prevented, the browser keeps it; inside an input the keymap was already silent. **(2) The chat bubble's text is crisp:** `.chatb .saytxt` weight 700, a brighter bone (#f4ecdc) and a tight dark halo instead of the wide red glow; the medallion and the clan mark keep their opacity (a test pins both). The whole-bubble keyframe opacity is unchanged — text and medallion share one element, so the glyphs were sharpened, not un-faded. **(3) One key cycles the phases:** `phaseNext` (default §) → Unlock → … → Discard → Unlock, from -1 it starts at Unlock; it goes THROUGH `activatePhase`, so the leave-phase check and the Unlock prompts apply as for F1–F5. § was the FIXED view-cycle key (with `, a dead key on Swedish keyboards, so § was the Swedish way in). Johan's call (alt. A): the view cycle (`cycleView`, default V) and the Tab flip (`activeHome`, default Tab) became rebindable ACTIONS with `when()` gates (`tableView`) — an inapplicable key falls through to the browser unprevented — and `nav:true`, so view/UI moves are not undo points (the fixed block never pushed undo; the keymap does, and `nav` keeps that true). `keyBindsReconcile(stored)`: a newcomer whose default the player had already bound elsewhere starts unbound — the player's binding wins, the same one-key-one-action rule `captureKey` enforces. The global handler is the extracted `keyDispatch(e)` (returns handled/not), which the suite drives with a plain `{key,target}` event. **(4) Tab never reached a bot offline, and the reason is in `pass()`:** the human's seat stays in `net.turnSeat` for the whole `hotRunBotTurns` sweep (it is written to the NEXT human only after the sweep), so `toggleActiveHome` read active = me and went home on every press. Now the sweep sets the acting bot as `turnSeat` while it plays (`updatePhaseGlow` follows) and restores the human's seat in `finally`, before `pass()` writes the next one. Consequence to know: during a bot's turn the phase bar is read-only (`pcother`) and PREY/PREDATOR count from the bot — bot turns ARE turns (v2.6.67). Online the server's `turn{seat}` already covers bot seats; read as working, not browser-verified this round. **(5) Esc on the L0 start screen painted the felt + pool globe:** the global Esc rung ran `goHome()` unconditionally → `switchView('l2')` → `enterL2()` drops `l0mode`. `goHome` is skipped while `tableIdle()` (the v2.6.79 idle predicate: no cards, no hotseat, no room); overlays and menus still close. The Esc handler is a document listener the harness cannot fire, so this one is a source pin. Gates: client 182/0 + 5 skipped (187 with the bot; +6 tests, destructive 6/6 vs v2.6.117; the v2.6.107 pin on weight 600 updated to 700 — it pins the SIZE), dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, endpoint 20/0 (+2 bot statics), canary skipped without the bot, B1 byte-identical 1 282 724, check-versions green. Browser check before purge: Enter in a hotseat with bots and online; § through a turn; V/Tab in a room; Esc on the start card.

> **16 September 2026 (THE PERMANENT RESUME — client v2.6.117 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** Johan's Resume button would not go away and opened a broken table. `closed` (v2.6.105) sets `net.status='off'` BEFORE `mpLeave` — deliberately — and `mpLeave`'s `hadGame` was `netGame()` = `inRoom() && deckUp`, so every table ended by Close table (or the server closing a bot room) kept its cards on the felt with `net.room` null; the minute autosave / `beforeunload` then wrote the online board to the BARE `elysium.game` key, the solo save nothing removes, and localStorage survives both a browser restart and a Cloudflare purge. `hadGame=!!net.deckUp` now; `mpTableGone(roomKey)` is the single clear+forget path, shared by `mpLeave` and the failed-rejoin `err` branch (same hole, older). Two behavioural tests drive `closed` and `err` on a dealt table and read the storage stub (`__ls`); destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.116. Gates: client 181/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1309541, check-versions green.

Tutorial stop `oh-tournlock` gone, the Helpers stop explains the lock. To bring the choice back: the label, the read in `mpCreate`, the tutorial stop — the comment at the label's old place lists them. > **16 September 2026 (THE ECHO, THE STOPWATCH AND THE X — client v2.6.115 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.115, pure client — purge):** three finds from Johan's test round. **The lobby X left the bot seated:** `netBotRemove` closed the bot's socket in the same breath as the kick, and a bare close is a DISCONNECT (`dropPlayer(conn,false)`, srv 1536) that keeps the seat for the reconnect window — which `kick` then refuses (srv 1027). The bot sends `leave` on its OWN socket now and we close only once the lobby push confirms the seat is gone; the host kick remains the fallback for an already-dead socket. No server change. **Your own chat bubbled twice under Show all:** the line has two sources (the send paint and the server echo), and v2.6.113 turned v2.6.50's hard-coded "not mine" test into a mode, which kept the intention and dropped the invariant it silently carried. `chatBubbleWanted(who, src)` drops the echo in every mode; the sender-side paint survives. **The Reaction window now stops when the table answers:** one `dfxCancel()`, called from `showSay`, from `cardFx` on a played card or a verb-bearing action clone (above the `animMode` returns, so animation-free and remote clients get it), and locally from the menu actions for the autoLock-off case. `tally` is deliberately excluded — the bot writes its stealth at every announce. Residual named: autoLock off + already-locked minion leaves remote screens counting. Gates: client 179/0, dispatch 47/0, protocol 6/0, canary 37/0, B1 byte-identical 1 277 347, check-versions green.

> **15 September 2026 (THE TIERS NEVER CROSS — client v2.6.114 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** pinned that a quick phrase cannot also pop the chat bubble — one caller asks for `.chatb`, `showSay` never mentions `chatBubble`, the chat handler never touches `SAY`. Corrects the v2.6.113 note: quick phrases have NO setting at all (the three-way is `conv.anim` over CARD animations); Johan considers the absence deliberate. The loud tier has TWO sources — `say{i}` and resolver verdicts — so a future gate must choose which it covers. §12.0 gains a what-governs-each table.

> **15 September 2026 (WHOSE BUBBLE — client v2.6.113 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** `conv.chatBubbles` (Hide mine / Show all / Hide all) in the PLAY HELPERS Advanced fold. The hard-coded “not mine” test moved out of the chat handler into the pure `chatBubbleWanted()` — a policy decision in a dispatcher is what kept it from being a setting. Two gates in order: `hx('chatFx')` (the table's, host-lockable, tournament-killed) then the local filter, which can only SUBTRACT. Quick phrases untouched.

> **15 September 2026 (ABSENT IS NOT OFF — client v2.6.112 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** `hx()` read a helper key MISSING from a locked policy as false. The server rebuilds `settings` from its own `HELPER_KEYS`, so `chatFx` (client v2.6.107, server srv-2.6.37) is absent from every srv-2.6.35 policy — the chat animation was dead on Johan's table. An unmentioned key falls through to the local setting now (`key in settings`); tournament still overrides first. Masked until v2.6.111 stopped the policy nulling itself. Also: your own chat has never bubbled by design (v2.6.50, receiver-side), and the bot panel moved to top-right, clear of the pool globe.

> **15 September 2026 (TWO NAMES FOR ONE THING — client v2.6.111 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** the s2c `helperPolicy` broadcast carries `policy` (srv 957) while `joined` carries `helperPolicy` (srv 411); the handler read only the second, so every confirmation of a policy NULLED it and `hx()` fell through to the player's local `conv.tournament` — a table created as "All helpers on" ran with every helper off. Reads `m.policy || m.helperPolicy` now: works against every deployed server, no restart. **Gate gap worth remembering:** the protocol lint diffs the doc against the CLIENT, never against what the SERVER sends, so an s2c field-name mismatch is invisible to every gate we have.

> **15 September 2026 (THE MIRROR BUG — client v2.6.110 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** Johan's first browser check, and it went red on the debug checkbox: greyed out on a non-tournament table. `botDebugAllowed()` read `conv.tournament` as a flat veto, but that is the player's PERSISTED preference, not this table's mode. The offline dropdown (`#offHelpers`) is the table's authority and was already checked; `conv.tournament` must only speak when no other authority is present. The irony: v2.6.109's comment named the first draft's bug ("a stubbed-out dropdown swallowed the conventions check") and shipped its mirror image (a conventions check that swallowed the dropdown). The harness gained an `_ids` map on the document stub so tests can inject a named element for `$()` and a `__doc` accessor so the test can reach it; the v2.6.108 debug test now covers both the with-dropdown and without-dropdown paths.

> **15 September 2026 (THE PANEL, REVIEWED — client v2.6.109 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** the pre-test review of v2.6.108 found three wiring gaps no source test covered: the panel never mounted ONLINE, the offline debug row only re-synced on a dropdown change, and the 4 Hz loop rebuilt the buttons every tick (click race — now shape-signature-guarded, countdown updated in place). `status()`/`step()` verified LIVE against a real held bot over hotBridge; the render loop hammered through hostile `status()` shapes without a throw. The visual result still needs a browser — the harness DOM is a Proxy stub.\n\n> **15 September 2026 (THE BOT PANEL — client v2.6.108 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.112, pure client — purge):** the last two client items from the online feedback round. A collapsible bot panel in the corner reads `status()` from every bot this tab owns (`hotBots` / `netBots`) — name, what it waits for, a countdown to `until`, and a step button labelled from `status().step`. All in-process, no wire change, host-only online. A hold shows an em-dash: bot 615 gives it `until:null` by design. `botPanelRead()` degrades every bad shape to a readable row rather than killing a 4 Hz loop. The offline ▶ sweep moved INTO the panel — `step()` pulls a wait forward, it does not play a turn. The panel joins `BOARD_CHROME`. Debug bot is a spawn option on both Add-bot paths, refused by `botDebugAllowed()` if ANY authority says tournament — unbranched and failing closed, since allowing wrongly opens a hand to the table. §12 records that both block shapes are parsed and neither is canonical, and that the undirected announce family is bot-emitted only. Consumed bot is v0.6.112.

> **14 September 2026 (THREE VOICES — client v2.6.107 / server v2.6.37 / bot v0.6.108, RESTART + purge):** typed chat gets its own calmer bubble. The three tiers already existed on the wire (`log` / `chat` / `say{i}`); `chat` and `say` just shared one visual. `sayBubble()` takes a `variant` now — one mounting path, one shared 3-bubble budget — and `.chatb` overrides only typography, measure and medallion size. New helper `chatFx`, which REQUIRES srv-2.6.37: `HELPER_DEFS` is mirrored by hand in the server's `HELPER_KEYS`, and `cleanHelperPolicy` builds its settings from the server list alone, so a client-only key is dropped from every host-locked policy. A test diffs the two lists both ways. New protocol §12.0 states what the three channels mean — the handoff for the bot's announce-then-ask double bubble.

> **14 September 2026 (THE CONTRACT ROUND — client v2.6.106 / server v2.6.36 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** the handoff to the sister project, contract first. `SAY` +2 APPENDED (8 `Any blocks?`, 9 `Any reaction?`) — never reorder, `say` carries an index and the bot mirrors the array. A Vote submenu under Actions: `Political action` is a true action (locks, closes the batch, carries the target suffix) so a bot's block step reaches it like a bleed; `Cast votes → For/Against` announces without locking. §12 now freezes the client-emitted minion action lines the bot grammar had as "unverified (main-project ask)", and new §12.1 freezes the `say{i}` table with its append-only and tolerate-unknown-index rules; a test diffs §12.1 against the live array.

> **14 September 2026 (CLOSE TABLE — client v2.6.105 / server v2.6.36 / bot v0.6.108, RESTART + purge):** ending a table is an intention now. New REF_VERB `closeRoom` → `closeRoomNow()` broadcasts `closed{msg}`, deletes the room, then closes every socket. `humansLeft(room)` is the server's first human/bot census (`p.bot` existed since July but nothing counted it) and counts ROSTER SEATS, never sockets — so a disconnect keeps its reconnect window while a real leave that takes the last non-bot player closes the room at once. The reaper gives a bot-holding room with no human online a 10-minute grace instead of the 60-minute rule. Client: `Host…` → `Close table…` (online only), and `Leave seat` asks a host with company Leave only / Close the table / Cancel; bots are not company. The `closed` handler goes `net.status='off'` before `mpLeave` so no rejoin loop chases a deleted room.

> **14 September 2026 (THE POLICY GOES BACK BEFORE THE DOOR — client v2.6.104 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** raised by Johan's standing "anything odd?" gate right after v2.6.103. Making the lock real (`allowLocal:false`) turned a harmless no-op into a live bug one call site away: a host leaving with bots still seated handed the remaining players a helper-locked table with nothing holding the lock. The restore could NOT be added to `netBotsTeardown`, which is where it looks like it belongs — `mpLeave` has already sent `leave` by that line and the server has dropped the connection from the room (srv 1261), so every REF_VERB answers 'Join a room first'. `mpLeave` calls `netBotHelperUnlock(true)` BEFORE the leave verb instead, and the unlock takes a quiet flag so a teardown does not toast. Residual, by design: a host who closes the TAB sends nothing, so the policy stays locked until someone changes it — same root as the room-survives-a-closed-tab case, and the durable fix belongs server-side in the Close table round.

> **14 September 2026 (ONE RULE FOR THE FELT — client v2.6.103 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.108, pure client — purge):** `#board` had two gesture handlers with different ideas of bare felt — the pan handler carried an exclusion list, the wheel handler carried none — so the pool globe's wheel reached the zoom. Both read one constant, `BOARD_CHROME`, via `isBoardChrome()` now: chrome and cards swallow the wheel, open board and the mats zoom. `#pool` / `#dockPool` got the `stopPropagation` their four siblings always had. Separately, `netBotHelperLock` sent `allowLocal:true` — the 'local' mode — so the bot helper lock had never locked anything; it calls `helperPolicyFor('all')` now. Consumed bot moves to v0.6.108 (PROJECT-MANIFEST §E2 was two behind), which also closed the hold-announcement gap the client suite pinned as a known failure.

> **13 September 2026 (THE TABLE WAS NEVER FULL — client v2.6.102 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's first browser-pass find, and a plain bug of mine: Add bot refused with "The table is full" for a host sitting alone. `netBotWhy` compared the player count against the lobby snapshot's `seats` — which is NOT a capacity. The server sets `seats: room.players.length` (srv 318) as the seat-picker's RANGE, so a lone host read 1 >= 1 and the gate closed. The real ceiling is `MAX_PLAYERS` (srv 56), enforced at join as `room.players.length >= MAX_PLAYERS` (srv 1072) with no filtering. The client now caps on `MP_MAX_PLAYERS`, a lockstep mirror, checked exactly as the server checks it. Walked 1→7 players: allowed through 5, refused at 6 with the server's own wording. Gates: client 145/0, destructive 1/1, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 243 921, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (THE HOLD BADGE FOLLOWS THE TABLE — client v2.6.101 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** The hold badge was gated on `localTable`, so the ONLINE bot tables added in v2.6.99 had no visible hold state at all — a forgotten "Hold on…" froze them with nothing on screen, the exact silence v2.6.96 closed offline. A hole introduced by omission, caught in Johan's post-delivery pass. The badge now follows any table that HAS bots: bridge seats offline, the server's own roster `bot` flag online (so it is true for every player, not only the host who added them). Online the name-keying solves itself — every human has their own client and speaks in their own name — so the rule is: you release your OWN hold with a real `It resolves` broadcast (a badge that only cleared local state would leave the bots held), and another player's hold is shown WITHOUT a Resume button, so a frozen table explains itself instead of offering a control that cannot work. Inbound `say` now feeds the badge, and leaving an online room clears it as `leaveHotseat` does offline. One deliberate tightening: tracking now requires the table to have bots, not merely to be local — a hold with nothing to hold is not state worth keeping. Two v2.6.96 tests were updated to that semantics. Gates: client 145/0, destructive 4/4, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 243 412, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (THE POLICY SNAPSHOT READS THE ROOM — client v2.6.100 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Caught in Johan's post-delivery 'anything unsure?' pass. `netBotHelperLock` remembered the helper policy by reading the Settings host-box checkboxes — which hold their HTML defaults if the host never opened that dialog — so removing the last bot could restore a policy the room never had. It now snapshots `net.helperPolicy`, the server-sent live policy (set at 8362 on join and 8581 on change), with the old read kept only as a fallback for the should-not-happen case of no policy pushed yet. Also verified while checking: `bot.close()` sets `_closing` and clears the reconnect timers, so a kicked bot with `reconnect:true` does not rejoin. Gates: client 141/0, destructive 1/1, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 241 263, check-versions green.

> **13 September 2026 (ONLINE BOTS FROM THE LOBBY — client v2.6.99 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's ask, so he can compare bot play online against the hotseat he tested offline. The host adds bots from the LOBBY, reactively, so a dropped or no-show player can be replaced mid-lobby. Each bot is a second browser WebSocket to the same server (`netBotDial` reads `net.addr`/`net.sec`), joining `net.room` with `net.pass` — entirely separate from `net.ws`. **No bridge is involved:** online the server IS the bus, so the hold, the referee verbs, board pubs and the fan-out are all native. That is what makes this the fidelity test for the bridge — a table that plays differently offline means the bridge differs, not the bot. Host-only, Full mode, Structured rooms only: `boardMode` is fixed at room creation (srv 1327) and no verb changes it, so a Classic room shows the reason on a disabled button rather than spawning a bot that will misplay. Helpers CAN change in the lobby (`setHelperPolicy` is a REF_VERB), so the first bot locks the policy to every helper on + no tournament mode, mirroring v2.6.93 offline, and removing the last bot restores what was there before. Seats are set on the bot's own socket with the same `seat` verb a human uses. Kicking a bot closes its socket too, or it would simply rejoin. Bots live in the host's tab and are torn down on leave. Same decks, personas, `paceMs 3100` and `askSecs 20` as offline, so the comparison is like-for-like. **Proved live against a real server** (localhost, real bot v0.6.107): two bots joined, took seats via the `seat` verb, the host's `start` started the game, and each bot's `this.table` read [1, 3] and [1, 2] — which also settles, by running it, the one claim the 12 Sep handoff had to mark as read-not-run. Also worth noting for the LOCAL server: until now there was no way at all to seat a bot there without running the bot's CLI by hand, one process per bot. Gates: client 140/0, destructive 5/5, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 241 213, check-versions green. No server change.

> **12 September 2026 (THE BOTS CAN SEE THE TABLE — client v2.6.98 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan's call, and the right one: blind bots make a test measure their card play rather than their decision logic, so you cannot tell what you are looking at. Measured before the change: after a full 2-bot hotseat game every bot's `this.table` was EMPTY — no seat at all. Online the server broadcasts every board push (srv line 1134) and hands a joiner every existing pub (398/1412), which is the input behind `_tableThreat` (who is the table threat), `_rushCandidates` (what there is to rush) and `_checkAshurTableWide` (Johan's July ask: react to what a HUMAN plays). Offline none of it had a source — `schedulePush`/`pushNow` return early when not `inRoom()`, and v2.6.95's bus carried log/say/chat/fx but deliberately not board. v2.6.98 puts board on the bus: each bot's pub fans out to the other bots, and the active human's board is pushed to every bot seat on the same 600 ms debounce as online, seeded at `startHotseat` the way the server seeds a joiner. Measured after: Bot1 sees seats [1, 3], Bot2 sees [1, 2], with real pools and card counts, and `_tableThreat` returns a real score instead of running on nothing. Gates: client 135/0 no skips, destructive 3/3, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 230 537, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE REFEREE VERBS REACH THE BOT — client v2.6.97 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan asked whether his long-standing rule — any player may control a bot seat's elements, since a headless bot can't model every triggered ability (v0.4.4, 13 July) — actually holds today. It held for CARDS: blood ±1, lock, flip, blue/green counters and torpor all ride `hotCtrl` on a bot seat, gated on the roster `bot` flag that `startHotseat` sets correctly. It did NOT hold for the seat-level referee verbs. `hostSetPoolHot` edited the stored pub while the bot kept its own `this.pool`, so the bot overwrote the edit on its next publish — measured against bot v0.6.107: pool set to 5, bot still holding 30, display back to 28 the moment it acted. `hostOustHot` likewise marked the roster while the bot played on. Both receivers (`forceSetPool`, `forceOust`) already existed on the bot; only the sender was missing, exactly as with the log fan-out. v2.6.97 adds `hotBotSeat(seat)` (roster bot AND connected on the bridge AND a local table) and routes both verbs through it. A drain to zero goes through the full oust path instead of `forceSetPool(0)`, because the bot's own clamp-and-oust would otherwise skip the predator's bounty and the roster flag and leave half a state. The quick Pool ± steps — built for political actions, which is exactly when a bot miscounts — are no longer online-only. Gates: client 132/0 no skips, destructive 4/4, canary 37/0, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 228 304, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE HOTSEAT BUS + THE VISIBLE HOLD — client v2.6.96 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.107, pure client — purge):** Johan uploaded bot v0.6.107 and asked what the CLIENT could do to make human-vs-bot play land well (he tests 1 human + 2 bots, then 2 + 2). Measuring against the real bot found the hotseat bridge was a STAR with no fan-out: the human's public log lines reached no bot (so a bleed never registered — the bot's L12 grammar reads those frozen lines), and two bots were mutually deaf, so v0.6.107's headline undirected reaction window could never fire. The fallback channel was shut too: `#chatRow` is hidden while `net.status==='off'`, so the interface the bots advertise in their opening line ("bleed N", "@Name bleed N") had no input offline. **v2.6.95** adds `hotFanOut()` — the human's lines ride `mpRelay` to every bot, each bot's log/say/chat/fx fans out to the OTHER bots, always excluding the sender. Loop safety is free from `log()`'s own contract: bot-injected lines are logged `localOnly=true` and never reach `mpRelay`. The chat line now exists offline and speaks onto the bus. ONE exception, found by soaking a 2-bot game to a deadlock in round 3: a BOT's "Hold on…" is not relayed to other bots — the bot filters `players[].bot` in `_holdHook` but NOT in the ask-window pause (`a.pausedBy`), so a bot could park another bot's undirected window with no timer and nothing to release it. Reported for the bot project; the bus filters it meanwhile. **v2.6.96** makes the hold visible and releasable: a badge names who holds and one click resumes, relaying `It resolves` PER HOLDER NAME (measured: Bob's ordinary release left the bots' holder set reading ["Alice"] forever, and `_pace()` + `hotRunBotTurns` both wait unbounded, so a forgotten hold froze the table with nothing on screen). Say bubbles now live 3 s for a quick phrase and up to 9 s for a long line; PREY/PREDATOR labels work at a hotseat. Gates: client 128/0 with NO skips (the bot file is present — canary 37/0), destructive 9/9, dispatch 44/0, protocol 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 226 382, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE SAY BUBBLE CLAMP — client v2.6.94 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan's report: a bot's initial chat message spilled outside the board on a laptop because `.sayb` had `white-space:nowrap` and `.saytxt` was a fixed 58px Cinzel. Now: `.sayb` gets `max-width:min(92vw,92%)` + `overflow-wrap:break-word` (no more nowrap); `.saytxt` uses `clamp(22px, 4.5vw, 58px)` and `.saywho` uses `clamp(12px, 1.4vw, 18px)` — the font scales with the viewport, so short quick-phrases ('Block!', 'Pass') stay big on desktop while a 120-char chat line wraps and shrinks on a laptop or phone. `chatBubble`'s 120-char JS guard stays. Gates: client 115/0 (+2; destructive vs v2.6.90), dispatch 44/0, protocol lint 6/0, B1 byte-identical 1 217 279, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE BOT LOCK — client v2.6.93 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan wants bots in a controlled environment until they graduate: when any offline seat is a bot, Board style is forced to Structured and Helpers to All helpers on, both selects greyed out (`syncOffBotLock()` in `renderOfflineSeats` + `openOffline`); removing the last bot restores the player's previous choice (the snapshot is idempotent — a second bot does not overwrite it with the forced value). `startOfflineFromCfg` reads the selects as always, so no special-case needed in the start path. Gates: client 113/0 (+3; destructive vs v2.6.90), dispatch 44/0, protocol lint 6/0, endpoint smoke 20/0 (+2 bot statics), canary skipped, B1 byte-identical 1 216 834, check-versions green.

> **12 September 2026 (THE TOURNAMENT OVERRIDE IS A SESSION — client v2.6.92 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, pure client — purge):** Johan's 'anything else odd?' gate caught it via the new header: a hotseat's Helpers choice (`startOfflineFromCfg`) and the tutorial's Classic rule wrote `conv.tournament` straight for the session, and `leaveHotseat` never wrote it back -- the v2.6.51 'session-only, no saveConv' held only until the NEXT `saveConv()` (any Settings change, a Lite|Full switch, dismissing the welcome bubble), which persisted the table's value as the player's preference; the Tournament checkbox flipped silently and the v2.6.91 header read '(custom)' after a Structured hotseat. Now `hotTournSession(val)` snapshots the player's own value once (`hotTournBefore`) and overrides; `leaveHotseat` calls `hotTournRestore()` beside the `helperAllOn=false` it always did; a deliberate edit of the Settings checkbox DURING a table updates the snapshot so the restore does not undo it; the resumed Classic tutorial (`tutGameResume`) applies the rule too -- it relied on the leak before. > **11 September 2026, latest (PER-ROOM REFEREE — client v2.6.83 / server v2.6.35 / bot v0.6.102, restart + purge):** Johan's clarification turned into the better model: the referee password belongs to the ROOM, set by the host at creation. **Server:** `create{refPass}` → `room.refHash`; `join{refPass}` → verified against the room (wrong key = err + the room-password strike; a room without one errs 'This room has no referee password') → the joiner is a watcher with `conn.ref=true`, `joined{ref:true}`, `watchers[]/watcherList[].ref`; the watcher dispatch routes `REF_VERBS` (the 15 host-gated verbs) to `GAME_HANDLERS` with a pseudo-p `{name:'⚖ '+wname, token:null, ref:true}`; every gate is `hostOrRef(conn, room, p)` (host token, operator `conn.admin`, or this room's referee — scoped to `conn.room===room`); `roomList.rooms[].ref` (the srv-2.6.34 server-level flag is gone, never deployed); `dropWatcher` clears `conn.ref`. The operator key (`--referee-pass`/`--admin-pass`, hello `admin`) stays for `--create-policy admin` and passes every gate. **Client:** Create game → Game settings gains 'Referee password' (`#mpRefRoom`, Full) → `refPass` on create; Join as referee sends `refPass` on the join itself (no hello, no key on the list request); `joined.ref` → `net.isRef`, and the client's host gates read `net.isHost||net.isRef` (seat menu moderation, the Host… menu, lobby Start + kick/promote/mute, 💾 matches, helper-policy push, the seating-panel spectator moderation); ⚖ marks referee'd rooms in the list and referees in the lobby/seating spectator lists; the Local server field is 'Server referee key' (operator only). `mpRefSync` no longer greys anything. **Tutorial:** oh-admin dropped, `oh-referee` (#mpRefRoom, after oh-tournlock) added; oj-spect and os-dialog rewritten. Dispatch 44/0 (the per-room test replaces the srv-2.6.34 one; destructive 1/1 vs srv-2.6.34), client 101/0 (+4; destructive 2/2 vs v2.6.82), protocol lint 6/0, smoke 20/0 (+2 bot statics), B1 byte-identical 1 204 907, check-versions green. Browser check: create with a referee password → ⚖ in the list → Join as referee → the Host menu and Start as a spectator; a plain spectator still chat-only.

> **THE SPLIT (9 Sep 2026) — read this before touching anything bot-shaped.** The Trainbot, the arena, `elysium-bot-table.js`, the `gen-*` trace tools, the cardfx pipeline, the arena decks, the bot suites and the bot design canon now live in the sister project **Elysium Bots** (its own CLAUDE.md, journal, context, learnings, changelog). This project owns the client (including hotBridge, the hotseat-bot UI and HOT_BOT_DECKS), the server (including the bot statics route), the harness, the client/server suites, the protocol, ops and these docs. **THE RULE: consume releases, never edit the other project's files.** The bot artifacts are NOT in this project's knowledge; the deploy bundle takes them from the Elysium Bots project's latest release (recorded in PROJECT-MANIFEST.md, "consumed releases"). The four client-suite tests that need `elysium-bot.js` are `testB`: they SKIP loudly when the file is absent (83/0 + 4 skipped) and run fully when Johan uploads the released bot beside the suite (87/0) — a session that touches hotBridge asks for it. `test-bot-canary.js` (the §12 grammar contract test) lives in BOTH projects and needs both files: here it skips loudly without the bot and runs 37/0 with the released bot uploaded — run it whenever a frozen log line changes. A change that needs both sides is TWO sessions: the contract (protocol/server/client) here first, released; the bot consumes next session — or bot first when backward-compatible. Never both in one round.

> **19 August 2026 (LIVE TELEMETRY — client v2.6.63 / server v2.6.30, pure client):** pinch crash (10× WebKit) root-caused — cancelSingles' synthetic pointercancels were swallowed by the tracker's OWN end(), emptying pts at activation (a[0].x on empty, then a[1].x on one-entry via the poisoned pinch flag, alternating 1:1); fixed with _synth flag + end() ignore + 3 guards. escapeHtml null-hardened (Firefox s-is-null). > **2 August 2026, later (VA REVIEW — Johan's utred-ask; bot v0.6.81, suite 656/0 ×3; the floor design source-verified and REVERSED):** the three declared uncertainties all resolved with positive findings, plus two real bugs. **(1) Verdict sender FOUND:** the actor bot itself sends the frozen `Block: Stealth A vs Intercept B` line and judges `iB >= sA` from the tally — proving first the wire constraint, then (via the rulebook) its resolution. **(2) THE REVERSAL:** the current vekn.net rulebook says stealth/intercept *"can decrease even below 0"* and the verdict is the raw `>=` comparison; FAQ 3.22 answers the exact flagged question (bare −1 intercept can NOT block 0 stealth) — v0.6.80's floor-at-0 was rules-wrong and was reversed the same day, before any arena data was built on it. The raw model: L12.block widened to `-?\d+` (−9 sanity cap, protocol doc §12 noted), `_setTallyB` raw (bare Christine writes b=−1 and her block correctly FAILS), potential/pick raw. **(3) The aus-peek under-claim was WRONG and closed:** `pending.peekSup` has carried the aus/AUS form since R3 — Dowager's +1 now applies on [aus]-peek blocks. **Bug 1 (phantom stealth):** `_bumpTallyA` accumulated on the clamped wire value, so Royce's −1 vanished under any stealth play (−1+1 showed 1) — fixed with the actor-side `_actStealthRaw` accumulator + the new `_announceTallyA` helper all 11 block-announce sites now route through. **Bug 2 (implicit-floor sibling):** `interceptPotential`'s `best = 0` seed hid a lone Christine's −1 — now −Infinity-seeded. Follow-on: the negative-stealth certainty guard precedes decide's `icp<=0` shortcut (Royce's tax enables CARDLESS blocks; the a≥0 hand-card-centric v1 stance stands unchanged); `_bumpTallyB` confirmed orphan (warning-commented). Rules-true consequences live: Christine cannot bare-block 0-stealth, Royce is cardlessly blockable by anyone, Christine CAN block Royce (−1 ≥ −1).
